I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory vyperlang__vyper__0.3. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Immutable variables used in the constructor
### Version Information

* vyper Version (output of `vyper --version`): 0.3.1
* OS: macOS
* Python Version (output of `python --version`): 3.8.9

### What's your issue about?

If you use immutable variables in the constructor the variable fills with garbage(in this simplified example it remains 0).

```py
# @version 0.3.1


A: immutable(uint256)
a: public(uint256)


@external
def __init__(_a: uint256):
    A = _a
    self.a = _a  # breaks with A


@external
@view
def a1() -> uint256:
    return A
```


### How can it be fixed?

1) Immutable variables usage in constructor could be restricted by the compiler or
2) somehow set the right value.

</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me carefully read and understand the issue:

1. The issue is about immutable variables in Vyper contracts
2. When immutable variables are used in the constructor (__init__), they don't get properly initialized and remain as garbage values (like 0)
3. In the example, variable A is declared as immutable(uint256), assigned _a in the constructor, but when accessed later via a1(), it returns garbage instead of the expected value
4. The regular state variable `a` works fine when assigned the same value
5. Two potential solutions are suggested:
   - Restrict usage of immutable variables in constructor 
   - Properly set the right value for immutable variables

Key technical details:
- Vyper version 0.3.1
- Variable A is immutable(uint256) 
- Method __init__ tries to assign A = _a
- Method a1() returns A but gets garbage
- Regular state variable assignment works: self.a = _a

The problem seems to be in how immutable variables are handled during contract deployment/initialization.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 1.1 Code Analysis
The provided Vyper code snippet shows:
- An immutable variable `A: immutable(uint256)` 
- A public state variable `a: public(uint256)`
- Constructor `__init__` that assigns both variables the same value `_a`
- A view function `a1()` that returns the immutable variable `A`

### 1.2 Technical Details
- **Variable name**: `A` (immutable), `a` (state variable)
- **Method names**: `__init__`, `a1`
- **Error manifestation**: Immutable variable contains garbage value (0) instead of assigned value
- **Version**: Vyper 0.3.1

### 1.3 Problem Explanation
The issue is that immutable variables in Vyper are not being properly initialized when assigned values in the constructor. While regular state variables work correctly, immutable variables retain garbage values even after explicit assignment in `__init__`.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
1. Create a Vyper contract with an immutable variable
2. Assign a value to the immutable variable in the constructor
3. Deploy the contract 
4. Call a function that returns the immutable variable value
5. Observe that it returns garbage (0) instead of the assigned value

### 1.5 Best Practices for Testing and Fixing
- Need to understand how Vyper handles immutable variable storage vs regular state variables
- Should examine the compilation/deployment process for immutables
- Fix should either properly initialize immutables or restrict their usage in constructors
- Must ensure backward compatibility and not break existing functionality

   360	            addr = Expr.parse_value_expr(self.expr.value, self.context)
</tool_response><|assistant|></think>This is interesting! The code shows that when accessing an immutable variable in the constructor (`is_constructor`), it should:
1. Store a memory position for later access
2. Return an LLLnode pointing to that memory location

But when accessing it outside the constructor, it calculates the position from codesize.

   178	        ["seq", ["label", "fallback"], fallback_lll],

   185	    # https://github.com/vyperlang/vyper/pull/2466#discussion_r722816358

   188	        # when we codecopy the runtime code to memory

   192	        data_section = []
   193	        for immutable in immutables:
   194	            # store each immutable at the end of the runtime code
   195	            memory_loc, offset = (
   196	                immutable.pos,
   197	                immutable.data_offset,
   198	            )
   199	            lhs = LLLnode.from_list(
   200	                ["add", start_pos + offset, "_lllsz"], typ=immutable.typ, location="memory"

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I understand the flow! The issue is that in the constructor, the immutable variable is stored in memory at some location (`memory_loc`), but during deployment, the system tries to copy from that memory location to the final code location. However, there might be a mismatch in when and how this memory location is populated.

Let me also check the data positions handling:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vyperlang__vyper__0.3/vyper/semantics/validation/data_positions.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/vyperlang__vyper__0.3/vyper/semantics/validation/data_positions.py:
     1	# TODO this doesn't really belong in "validation"
     2	import math
     3	from typing import Dict, List
     4	
     5	from vyper import ast as vy_ast
     6	from vyper.exceptions import StorageLayoutException
     7	from vyper.semantics.types.bases import CodeOffset, StorageSlot
     8	from vyper.typing import StorageLayout
     9	
    10	
    11	def set_data_positions(
    12	    vyper_module: vy_ast.Module, storage_layout_overrides: StorageLayout = None
    13	) -> StorageLayout:
    14	    """
    15	    Parse the annotated Vyper AST, determine data positions for all variables,
    16	    and annotate the AST nodes with the position data.
    17	
    18	    Arguments
    19	    ---------
    20	    vyper_module : vy_ast.Module
    21	        Top-level Vyper AST node that has already been annotated with type data.
    22	    """
    23	    set_code_offsets(vyper_module)
    24	    return (
    25	        set_storage_slots_with_overrides(vyper_module, storage_layout_overrides)
    26	        if storage_layout_overrides is not None
    27	        else set_storage_slots(vyper_module)
    28	    )
    29	
    30	
    31	class StorageAllocator:
    32	    """
    33	    Keep track of which storage slots have been used. If there is a collision of
    34	    storage slots, this will raise an error and fail to compile
    35	    """
    36	
    37	    def __init__(self):
    38	        self.occupied_slots: Dict[int, str] = {}
    39	
    40	    def reserve_slot_range(self, first_slot: int, n_slots: int, var_name: str) -> None:
    41	        """
    42	        Reserves `n_slots` storage slots, starting at slot `first_slot`
    43	        This will raise an error if a storage slot has already been allocated.
    44	        It is responsibility of calling function to ensure first_slot is an int
    45	        """
    46	        list_to_check = [x + first_slot for x in range(n_slots)]
    47	        self._reserve_slots(list_to_check, var_name)
    48	
    49	    def _reserve_slots(self, slots: List[int], var_name: str) -> None:
    50	        for slot in slots:
    51	            self._reserve_slot(slot, var_name)
    52	
    53	    def _reserve_slot(self, slot: int, var_name: str) -> None:
    54	        if slot < 0 or slot >= 2 ** 256:
    55	            raise StorageLayoutException(
    56	                f"Invalid storage slot for var {var_name}, out of bounds: {slot}"
    57	            )
    58	        if slot in self.occupied_slots:
    59	            collided_var = self.occupied_slots[slot]
    60	            raise StorageLayoutException(
    61	                f"Storage collision! Tried to assign '{var_name}' to slot {slot} but it has "
    62	                f"already been reserved by '{collided_var}'"
    63	            )
    64	        self.occupied_slots[slot] = var_name
    65	
    66	
    67	def set_storage_slots_with_overrides(
    68	    vyper_module: vy_ast.Module, storage_layout_overrides: StorageLayout
    69	) -> StorageLayout:
    70	    """
    71	    Parse module-level Vyper AST to calculate the layout of storage variables.
    72	    Returns the layout as a dict of variable name -> variable info
    73	    """
    74	
    75	    ret: Dict[str, Dict] = {}
    76	    reserved_slots = StorageAllocator()
    77	
    78	    # Search through function definitions to find non-reentrant functions
    79	    for node in vyper_module.get_children(vy_ast.FunctionDef):
    80	        type_ = node._metadata["type"]
    81	
    82	        # Ignore functions without non-reentrant
    83	        if type_.nonreentrant is None:
    84	            continue
    85	
    86	        variable_name = f"nonreentrant.{type_.nonreentrant}"
    87	
    88	        # re-entrant key was already identified
    89	        if variable_name in ret:
    90	            _slot = ret[variable_name]["slot"]
    91	            type_.set_reentrancy_key_position(StorageSlot(_slot))
    92	            continue
    93	
    94	        # Expect to find this variable within the storage layout override
    95	        if variable_name in storage_layout_overrides:
    96	            reentrant_slot = storage_layout_overrides[variable_name]["slot"]
    97	            # Ensure that this slot has not been used, and prevents other storage variables
    98	            # from using the same slot
    99	            reserved_slots.reserve_slot_range(reentrant_slot, 1, variable_name)
   100	
   101	            type_.set_reentrancy_key_position(StorageSlot(reentrant_slot))
   102	
   103	            ret[variable_name] = {
   104	                "type": "nonreentrant lock",
   105	                "location": "storage",
   106	                "slot": reentrant_slot,
   107	            }
   108	        else:
   109	            raise StorageLayoutException(
   110	                f"Could not find storage_slot for {variable_name}. "
   111	                "Have you used the correct storage layout file?",
   112	                node,
   113	            )
   114	
   115	    # Iterate through variables
   116	    for node in vyper_module.get_children(vy_ast.AnnAssign):
   117	
   118	        # Ignore immutable parameters
   119	        if node.get("annotation.func.id") == "immutable":
   120	            continue
   121	
   122	        type_ = node.target._metadata["type"]
   123	
   124	        # Expect to find this variable within the storage layout overrides
   125	        if node.target.id in storage_layout_overrides:
   126	            var_slot = storage_layout_overrides[node.target.id]["slot"]
   127	            # Calculate how many storage slots are required
   128	            storage_length = math.ceil(type_.size_in_bytes / 32)
   129	            # Ensure that all required storage slots are reserved, and prevents other variables
   130	            # from using these slots
   131	            reserved_slots.reserve_slot_range(var_slot, storage_length, node.target.id)
   132	            type_.set_position(StorageSlot(var_slot))
   133	
   134	            ret[node.target.id] = {"type": str(type_), "location": "storage", "slot": var_slot}
   135	        else:
   136	            raise StorageLayoutException(
   137	                f"Could not find storage_slot for {node.target.id}. "
   138	                "Have you used the correct storage layout file?",
   139	                node,
   140	            )
   141	
   142	    return ret
   143	
   144	
   145	def set_storage_slots(vyper_module: vy_ast.Module) -> StorageLayout:
   146	    """
   147	    Parse module-level Vyper AST to calculate the layout of storage variables.
   148	    Returns the layout as a dict of variable name -> variable info
   149	    """
   150	    # Allocate storage slots from 0
   151	    # note storage is word-addressable, not byte-addressable
   152	    storage_slot = 0
   153	
   154	    ret: Dict[str, Dict] = {}
   155	
   156	    for node in vyper_module.get_children(vy_ast.FunctionDef):
   157	        type_ = node._metadata["type"]
   158	        if type_.nonreentrant is None:
   159	            continue
   160	
   161	        variable_name = f"nonreentrant.{type_.nonreentrant}"
   162	
   163	        # a nonreentrant key can appear many times in a module but it
   164	        # only takes one slot. after the first time we see it, do not
   165	        # increment the storage slot.
   166	        if variable_name in ret:
   167	            _slot = ret[variable_name]["slot"]
   168	            type_.set_reentrancy_key_position(StorageSlot(_slot))
   169	            continue
   170	
   171	        type_.set_reentrancy_key_position(StorageSlot(storage_slot))
   172	
   173	        # TODO this could have better typing but leave it untyped until
   174	        # we nail down the format better
   175	        ret[variable_name] = {
   176	            "type": "nonreentrant lock",
   177	            "location": "storage",
   178	            "slot": storage_slot,
   179	        }
   180	
   181	        # TODO use one byte - or bit - per reentrancy key
   182	        # requires either an extra SLOAD or caching the value of the
   183	        # location in memory at entrance
   184	        storage_slot += 1
   185	
   186	    for node in vyper_module.get_children(vy_ast.AnnAssign):
   187	
   188	        if node.get("annotation.func.id") == "immutable":
   189	            continue
   190	
   191	        type_ = node.target._metadata["type"]
   192	        type_.set_position(StorageSlot(storage_slot))
   193	
   194	        # this could have better typing but leave it untyped until
   195	        # we understand the use case better
   196	        ret[node.target.id] = {"type": str(type_), "location": "storage", "slot": storage_slot}
   197	
   198	        # CMC 2021-07-23 note that HashMaps get assigned a slot here.
   199	        # I'm not sure if it's safe to avoid allocating that slot
   200	        # for HashMaps because downstream code might use the slot
   201	        # ID as a salt.
   202	        storage_slot += math.ceil(type_.size_in_bytes / 32)
   203	
   204	    return ret
   205	
   206	
   207	def set_calldata_offsets(fn_node: vy_ast.FunctionDef) -> None:
   208	    pass
   209	
   210	
   211	def set_memory_offsets(fn_node: vy_ast.FunctionDef) -> None:
   212	    pass
   213	
   214	
   215	def set_code_offsets(vyper_module: vy_ast.Module) -> None:
   216	
   217	    offset = 0
   218	    for node in vyper_module.get_children(
   219	        vy_ast.AnnAssign, filters={"annotation.func.id": "immutable"}
   220	    ):
   221	        type_ = node._metadata["type"]
   222	        type_.set_position(CodeOffset(offset))
   223	
   224	        offset += math.ceil(type_.size_in_bytes / 32) * 32
   225

"""
Script to reproduce the immutable variable issue in constructor.
"""

import vyper

# Test code from the issue description
test_code = """
# @version 0.3.1

A: immutable(uint256)
a: public(uint256)

@external
def __init__(_a: uint256):
    A = _a
    self.a = _a  # breaks with A

@external
@view
def a1() -> uint256:
    return A
"""

def test_immutable_issue():
    print("Testing immutable variable issue...")
    
    # Compile the contract
    try:
        compiled = vyper.compile_codes({"test.vy": test_code}, output_formats=["bytecode", "abi"])
        print("Compilation successful!")
        
        # Get the contract class
        from vyper import compile_code
        from eth_tester import EthereumTester
        from web3 import Web3
        
        # Setup web3 and tester
        tester = EthereumTester()
        w3 = Web3(Web3.EthereumTesterProvider(tester))
        
        # Compile and deploy
        abi = compile_code(test_code, ['abi'])['abi']
        bytecode = compile_code(test_code, ['bytecode'])['bytecode']
        
        # Create contract factory
        contract_factory = w3.eth.contract(abi=abi, bytecode=bytecode)
        
        # Deploy with value 42
        tx_hash = contract_factory.constructor(42).transact({'from': w3.eth.accounts[0]})
        tx_receipt = w3.eth.wait_for_transaction_receipt(tx_hash)
        
        # Get deployed contract
        contract_address = tx_receipt.contractAddress
        contract = w3.eth.contract(address=contract_address, abi=abi)
        
        # Test the values
        print(f"State variable a: {contract.functions.a().call()}")
        print(f"Immutable variable A via a1(): {contract.functions.a1().call()}")
        
        # Check if they match
        state_val = contract.functions.a().call()
        immutable_val = contract.functions.a1().call()
        
        if state_val == immutable_val:
            print("✅ SUCCESS: Both variables have the same value")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ ISSUE REPRODUCED: Immutable variable has different/garbage value")
            print(f"   Expected: {state_val}")
            print(f"   Got: {immutable_val}")
            return False
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during test: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

"""
Exact reproduction of the issue as described.
"""

import vyper
from vyper import compile_code
from eth_tester import EthereumTester
from web3 import Web3

# Exact code from issue description
test_code = """
# @version 0.3.2


A: immutable(uint256)
a: public(uint256)


@external
def __init__(_a: uint256):
    A = _a
    self.a = _a  # breaks with A


@external
@view
def a1() -> uint256:
    return A
"""

def test_exact_issue():
    print("Testing exact issue reproduction...")
    
    try:
        # Setup web3 and tester
        tester = EthereumTester()
        w3 = Web3(Web3.EthereumTesterProvider(tester))
        
        # Compile  
        abi = compile_code(test_code, ['abi'])['abi']
        bytecode = compile_code(test_code, ['bytecode'])['bytecode']
        
        print(f"ABI: {abi}")
        print(f"Bytecode length: {len(bytecode)}")
        
        # Create contract factory
        contract_factory = w3.eth.contract(abi=abi, bytecode=bytecode)
        
        # Deploy with value 100
        tx_hash = contract_factory.constructor(100).transact({'from': w3.eth.accounts[0]})
        tx_receipt = w3.eth.wait_for_transaction_receipt(tx_hash)
        
        # Get deployed contract
        contract_address = tx_receipt.contractAddress
        contract = w3.eth.contract(address=contract_address, abi=abi)
        
        # Test the values
        state_val = contract.functions.a().call()
        immutable_val = contract.functions.a1().call()
        
        print(f"State variable a: {state_val}")
        print(f"Immutable variable A via a1(): {immutable_val}")
        
        if state_val == immutable_val:
            print("✅ No issue detected - both values match")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ Issue reproduced - values don't match!")
            print(f"   Expected: {state_val}")
            print(f"   Got: {immutable_val}")
            return False
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during test: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""

# Exact code from issue description

# @version 0.3.2

A: immutable(uint256)
a: public(uint256)

@external
def __init__(_a: uint256):
    A = _a
    self.a = _a

@external
@view
def a1() -> uint256:
    return A
"""

def debug_compilation():
    print("Debugging compilation process...")
    
    try:
        # Compile with different outputs
        outputs = compile_code(test_code, ['abi', 'bytecode', 'asm', 'layout'])
        print("=== ABI ===")
        print(outputs['abi'])

        lll_str = str(lll_output['lll'])
        print(lll_str[:1000] + "..." if len(lll_str) > 1000 else lll_str)
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during compilation: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

Let me re-read the issue:

The user is saying that in their case, the immutable variable remains 0 instead of getting the assigned value. This could happen if:

   412	        _dbg_expr = target

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>So `parse_variable_location` just calls the regular `Expr` constructor and gets the `lll_node`. This means that when we do `A = _a` in the constructor, it goes through the same path as reading `A`, which is the code I saw earlier.

Let me trace through what should happen:
1. In constructor: `A = _a` 
2. The left side `A` gets parsed via `Expr` → goes to the immutable branch → `is_constructor` is True → returns memory location node
3. The right side `_a` gets parsed normally
4. `make_setter` is called to set the memory location

Then later:
1. Outside constructor: `return A`
2. The `A` gets parsed via `Expr` → goes to the immutable branch → `is_constructor` is False → returns code location node

The issue might be in step 4 of the first part - when copying from memory to code section during deployment. Let me look at that logic again in module.py:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vyperlang__vyper__0.3/vyper/codegen/module.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>view</arg_value><arg_key>view_range</arg_key><arg_value>[190, 225]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Here's the result of running `cat -n` on /workspace/vyperlang__vyper__0.3/vyper/codegen/module.py:

- When accessing immutable variable outside constructor: returns a code location node

2. Right side `_a` gets processed by Expr → returns value node
3. make_setter is called

But when we later do `return A`:
1. `A` gets processed by Expr → if in constructor, returns SAME memory location node

So the same memory location should be used. This looks correct.

"""
Complex test that might trigger the immutable issue.

import vyper
from vyper import compile_code
from eth_tester import EthereumTester
from web3 import Web3

# More complex code that might trigger the issue
complex_code = """
# @version 0.3.2

A: immutable(uint256)
B: immutable(uint256) 
C: immutable(uint256)
a: public(uint256)
b: public(uint256)
c: public(uint256)

@external
def __init__(_a: uint256, _b: uint256, _c: uint256):
    A = _a
    B = _b  
    C = _c
    self.a = _a
    self.b = _b
    self.c = _c

@external
@view
def get_a() -> uint256:
    return A

@external
@view
def get_b() -> uint256:
    return B

@external
@view
def get_c() -> uint256:
    return C

@external
@view
def get_all() -> (uint256, uint256, uint256):
    return A, B, C
"""

def test_complex_case():
    print("Testing complex immutable case...")
    
    try:
        # Setup web3 and tester
        tester = EthereumTester()
        w3 = Web3(Web3.EthereumTesterProvider(tester))
        
        # Compile  
        abi = compile_code(complex_code, ['abi'])['abi']
        bytecode = compile_code(complex_code, ['bytecode'])['bytecode']
        
        # Create contract factory
        contract_factory = w3.eth.contract(abi=abi, bytecode=bytecode)
        
        # Deploy with values
        tx_hash = contract_factory.constructor(100, 200, 300).transact({'from': w3.eth.accounts[0]})
        tx_receipt = w3.eth.wait_for_transaction_receipt(tx_hash)
        
        # Get deployed contract
        contract_address = tx_receipt.contractAddress
        contract = w3.eth.contract(address=contract_address, abi=abi)
        
        # Test the values
        print("Testing individual getters:")
        state_a = contract.functions.a().call()
        state_b = contract.functions.b().call() 
        state_c = contract.functions.c().call()
        immutable_a = contract.functions.get_a().call()
        immutable_b = contract.functions.get_b().call()
        immutable_c = contract.functions.get_c().call()
        
        print(f"State variables: a={state_a}, b={state_b}, c={state_c}")
        print(f"Immutable variables: A={immutable_a}, B={immutable_b}, C={immutable_c}")
        
        # Test tuple getter
        print("Testing tuple getter:")
        all_immutables = contract.functions.get_all().call()
        print(f"All immutables: {all_immutables}")
        
        # Check consistency
        success = (state_a == immutable_a and 
                  state_b == immutable_b and 
                  state_c == immutable_c and
                  all_immutables == [100, 200, 300])
        
        if success:
            print("✅ Complex case works correctly")
            return True
        else:
            print("❌ Complex case failed!")
            return False
            
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during test: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

   459	            # Restrict usage of immutable variables in constructor to prevent garbage values
   460	            # See issue: https://github.com/vyperlang/vyper/issues/XXXX
   461	            if node.get_ancestor(vy_ast.FunctionDef).get("name") == "__init__":
   462	                raise ImmutableViolation("Immutable variables cannot be used in constructors. "
   463	                                       "Consider using regular state variables instead.", node)
   464	            if self._modification_count:
   465	                raise ImmutableViolation(
   466	                    "Immutable value cannot be modified after assignment", node
   467	                )
   468	            self._modification_count += 1
   469	
   470	        if isinstance(node, vy_ast.AugAssign):
   471	            self.validate_numeric_op(node)
   472

But let me think about the core issue. The problem is that `rhs = LLLnode.from_list(memory_loc, typ=immutable.typ, location="memory")` reads from `memory_loc`, which should be where the constructor stored the value.

Where is `memory_loc` set? Looking at expr.py line 330: `self.context.global_ctx._globals[self.expr.id].pos = memory_loc`.

So during the assignment `A = _a` in the constructor:
1. The left side `A` gets processed, allocates a memory location, and stores it in `immutable.pos`  
2. The assignment stores the value at that memory location

During deployment:
1. The code copies from `immutable.pos` (the memory location) to the final code location

This should work. Unless... the memory location allocated during compilation is not the same as the one used during runtime execution.

Actually, let me think about this differently. What if the issue is that memory gets reused or overwritten between when the constructor stores the value and when the deployment code reads it?

Wait, let me check if there's a fundamental misunderstanding. Let me look at when exactly the copying happens:

Looking at lines 215-223 in module.py:
```python
o.append(
    [
        "with",
        "_lllsz",  # keep size of runtime bytecode in sz var
        ["lll", start_pos, runtime],  # store runtime code at `start_pos`
        # sequence of copying immutables, with final action of returning the runtime code
        ["seq", *data_section, ["return", start_pos, ["add", data_section_size, "_lllsz"]]],
    ]
)
```

So the sequence is:
1. Store runtime code at `start_pos`  
2. Copy immutables (data_section)
3. Return the combined code

The constructor execution happens as part of step 1, when the `runtime` code is executed via `["lll", start_pos, runtime]`. Then step 2 copies the values.

This looks correct. So why would it fail?

    68	        """
    69	        Allocate `size` bytes in memory.
    70	
    71	        *** No guarantees are made that allocated memory is clean! ***
    72	
    73	        If no memory was previously de-allocated, memory is expanded
    74	        and the free memory pointer is increased.
    75	
    76	        If sufficient space is available within de-allocated memory, the lowest
    77	        available offset is returned and that memory is now marked as allocated.
    78	
    79	        Arguments
    80	        ---------

    82	            The number of bytes to allocate. Must be divisible by 32.
    83	
    84	        Returns

    86	        int
    87	            Start offset of the newly allocated memory.
    88	        """
    89	        if size % 32 != 0:
    90	            raise CompilerPanic(f"tried to allocate {size} bytes, only multiples of 32 supported.")
    91	
    92	        # check for deallocated memory prior to expanding
    93	        for i, free_memory in enumerate(self.deallocated_mem):
    94	            if free_memory.size == size:
    95	                del self.deallocated_mem[i]
    96	                return free_memory.position
    97	            if free_memory.size > size:
    98	                return free_memory.partially_allocate(size)
    99	
   100	        # if no deallocated slots are available, expand memory
   101	        return self.expand_memory(size)

This confirms that my fix is needed, but my current fix isn't sufficient. The problem is that when there are multiple immutables, they might be getting the same memory location or conflicting with each other.

Let me revise my fix. Instead of checking if `var.pos` is set, I should make sure that for each immutable variable, we consistently use the same memory location throughout the constructor.

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>So the test assigns:
- `a = 3` (uint256) 
- `b = "0xEeeeeEeeeEeEeeEeEeEeeEEEeeeeEeeeeeeeEEeE"` (address)
- `c = "Hello world"` (String[64])

But the result is `[0, None, 'Hello world']` - so `a` becomes 0, `b` becomes None, but `c` keeps its value.

This suggests that the issue is with the ORDER or the way memory locations are being managed. The last assigned immutable works, but the earlier ones don't.

Looking at the copying logic in module.py, it sorts immutables by their `pos`:
```python
immutables = sorted(immutables, key=lambda imm: imm.pos)
```

So the copying order depends on the memory positions. Maybe there's an issue with how the positions are being calculated or stored.

Let me look at this from a different angle. Instead of trying to fix the memory management, let me make sure that each immutable gets a unique, non-conflicting memory location.

The issue might be that `new_variable` is allocating overlapping or conflicting memory locations. Let me check if there's a pattern.

Actually, let me add some debug output to understand what's happening. But first, let me understand the root cause by looking at the issue more carefully.

The fact that only the LAST assigned immutable works suggests that memory is being overwritten. When we do:
1. `a = 3` - stores 3 at memory location X
2. `b = addr` - stores addr at memory location Y  
3. `c = "Hello world"` - stores string at memory location Z

But during deployment copying, only Z has the correct value. This suggests that X and Y are being overwritten or corrupted.

One possibility is that the memory allocator is reusing memory locations, or there's some cleanup happening between assignments.

But looking at the `allocate_memory` function, it should provide non-overlapping memory locations.

Let me think of a different approach. Maybe the issue is not in the expr.py logic but in how the global context tracks the immutables.

Looking at the copying logic in module.py again:
```python
for immutable in immutables:
    memory_loc, offset = (
        immutable.pos,  # This is set in expr.py
        immutable.data_offset,
    )
    lhs = LLLnode.from_list(
        ["add", start_pos + offset, "_lllsz"], typ=immutable.typ, location="memory"
    )
    rhs = LLLnode.from_list(memory_loc, typ=immutable.typ, location="memory")
    # ...
```

So it's copying FROM `memory_loc` (which should be where the constructor stored the value) TO the final code location.

The issue is that `memory_loc` doesn't contain the right value.

Maybe the problem is that the memory locations are being allocated AFTER the values are supposed to be stored, or there's a timing issue.

Actually, let me look at when exactly `Expr` is called for the left side of assignments. 

When we do `a = _a`, the parser needs to:
1. Parse the left side `a` - this calls `Expr` which allocates memory and sets `immutable.pos`  
2. Parse the right side `_a` - this creates the value to be stored
3. Generate code to store the value at the memory location

So the memory location is allocated before the assignment happens. This should be fine.

But wait, what if there are MULTIPLE references to the same immutable variable? For example, what if we read the variable somewhere?

Actually, in the failing test, each immutable is only assigned once, so that's not the issue.

Let me try a different approach. Instead of trying to debug the complex memory management, let me make a simpler fix that ensures each immutable gets a dedicated, non-conflicting memory area.

One approach is to pre-allocate memory locations for all immutables at the beginning, rather than allocating them on-demand during expression parsing.

But that would require bigger changes. Let me try a simpler approach - make sure that the memory locations used for immutables are allocated at predictable, non-conflicting locations.

Actually, let me look at the issue from the perspective of what the copying code expects vs what the assignment code provides.

The copying code expects to find the immutable values at `immutable.pos`. The assignment code should store the values at `immutable.pos`. 

If the test shows that only the last one works, it suggests that:
1. All assignments are storing values at the right locations initially
2. But something is corrupting or overwriting the earlier locations before copying happens

Or alternatively:
1. The assignments are not storing values at the expected locations
2. The copying is reading from wrong locations

Actually, let me look at the issue one more time. The problem might be much simpler. 

Looking at the original code:
```python
memory_loc = self.context.new_variable(f"#immutable_{self.expr.id}", var.typ)
self.context.global_ctx._globals[self.expr.id].pos = memory_loc
```

Each time this code runs for the same variable (if the variable is referenced multiple times), it allocates a NEW memory location and OVERWRITES the old one in `immutable.pos`.

But in the failing test, each immutable is only assigned once, so that shouldn't be the issue.

Wait, let me re-read the test. The test assigns three different immutables: `a`, `b`, and `c`. Each one should get its own memory location. But maybe there's an interaction.

Actually, let me step back and realize that maybe the issue is that I'm overthinking this. Since I've confirmed that there IS a real bug (the test fails), and I have a clear symptom (only the last assigned immutable works), let me make a targeted fix.

The simplest fix is to ensure that each immutable variable gets a consistent, unique memory location that doesn't change. The current code allocates a new memory location each time the variable is processed, but only the last allocation is stored.
